[Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

In this room you see: Seren the Human,  Lilia the Fae, 1st urdaian city guard

[Merchant Square]
The corner of the cobblestone square comes to a halt in front of a pillared, marble building accented by tall trees braided with wildflowers. The moderate sized building frames a shiny brass door on which the words "Urda Merchants Council" are etched. Bushes trimmed and cut into various shapes, edge the buildings face. A small path to the northeast, lined with black wrought iron lamps leads towards a beautiful courtyard.

[Courtyard]
A shimmering fountain expels water in an umbrella shaped spray. Small fish of gold, red, yellow and blue dart about playfully beneath it. Green lily pads and various other pond flora float delicately amongst the water  occasionally playing host to frogs and turtles that wander through. Three large benches made of wood and stone have been placed around the fountains circumference. A elegant building sitting on the south side of the courtyard displays a black marble sign etched in gold lettering which reads "Bank of Urda."

[Bank of Urda]
urda
Ornate marble floors intersect with gray marble walls lit by highly polished copper oil lamps. Several counters line the eastern and western walls organized neatly with parchment and quills. Bank tellers sit behind clean counters counting coins for patrons and fetching bank boxes. A city guard sits beside the frequently opening and closing brass door on the northern wall.

[Merchants Council]
A large crystal chandelier casts a bright light upon a pentagon shaped room. Intricate marble molding accentuates smoothed and polished granite walls, which merge perfectly into shining black marble floors. Elegant tapestries illustrate craftsman immersed in their trades. [Urda Town Square Center]
On this site, a fierce battle for control of the great city was waged, and on this site is where its citizens now celebrate that victory. Statues of the fallen heroes, and great leaders of the alliance adorn the tops of grand marble archways that scatter over the square. They stand ever vigilant against any unseen enemy. The people of Urda flock here to barter and trade, to converse with friends, or just relax near the fountain and have a game of chess. Benches and tables dot the landscape near the edge of the fountain. Hawkers of all type roll through this area with their wares, hoping to make a few coins. The many languages of races and guilds reverberate off the storefronts of the surrounding Merchant Square where a constant foot traffic ebbs and flows, making this the heart of the city.

The area is illuminated.

Intricate marble molding accentuates smoothed and polished granite walls, which merge perfectly into shining black marble floors. Elegant tapestries illustrate craftsman immersed in their trades. A solid red granite, circular table with seven accompanying cushioned chairs marks the rooms center. Ornately etched glass display cases showcase wares of all sorts, some old and some new. The western wall hosts a large wooden bookshelf which houses several books dedicated to the trades.

[Chamber of Runes]
The misty purple-gray portal leads into a dusky, twilight-hued chamber. Orbs of smoke float within the room by the power of an unseen force, shrouding the lick of flame within them, making the room dim. A sturdy, violet sapphire inlaid table, lacquered with a mahogany finish, stands at center. High-back chairs circle the table as glyphs, sigils, and scorch marks glow with a magical fire, etched onto every inch of the table. Thick velvet draperies line the walls of the chamber, centering upon a stone statue of Karishta; one foot daintily outstretched to touch onto the surface of Aedin, just beyond the shroud.

[Corner of Ivy Park]
Ivy Park dead ends at the Skyline Museum of Modern Magic which looms to the north. [Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library - Entrance]
The high vaulted white plaster ceilings are a stark contrast to the deeply stained wood walls. The Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library is famous for its collection of reading materials covering most subjects, and from all over the Isles of Aedin. Anyone who has spent time and energy on a higher education has spent many hours studying within the well-restored walls of the great library of knowledge.

[Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library - Entrance]

[Umbral Rook University - Astral Attunement]
A large plain lecture hall with white plaster walls and solid oak trim is filled with simple wooden desks and chairs in lines of ten desks wide, and ten desks long. [Umbral Rook University - History and Geography]
One of the most interesting courses of study at the university is that of the complex history of the Isles of Aedin, and also how it has affected changes in the landscape, terrain, and borders of the civilizations, tribes, and cultures of the different islands. This large hall is mostly wood benches sitting atop wood risers and facing downwards towards a small recessed landing in front and along the northern wall. Behind a large wood podium in the center of the landing is a giant wall map of the Isles of Aedin complete with modern borders, boundaries, cities and roads intact. Large sections of the isle of Windgard on the wall map have been labeled as "wild", or "unexplored", or "destroyed". Lining the western and eastern walls are deeply stained ceiling high wooden bookshelves filled with books, tomes, manuscripts, maps, scrolls, and journals of those who have journeyed far and wide, and cared to scribe about it after wards. A pair of rolling ladders allows for access to the upper shelves. Stacked along the rear southern wall are several wooden folding chairs which can be used in case of seating overflow during a particularly popular lecture or famous explorer's appearance. A large brass plaque in the center of one wall reads, "School of History and Geography."

The area is illuminated.

[Umbral Rook University - Side Hall]
The billowing domed white canvas canopy overhead allows for a bit of shade during a hot day of walking to and from classes or halls. [Umbral Rook University - Office of Registration, Reception]

[Umbral Rook University - Science and Magic]
Several small wooden desks complete with chairs form several rows facing the western wall in this large lecture hall. Along the entire western wall is one long table with only a small space behind it for the instructor to pace back and forth while lecturing. Sitting on the long display table is a myriad of various crystals, rocks, prisms, magic symbols, vials, elixirs, skulls, dissected animals, and small clay bowls and dishes filled with powders, dust, particles, and other materials. A small stone fireplace in the northern wall burns brightly with glowing embers, and boils a small pot hanging from the blackened steel arm of the open damper. A large green chalk board hung on the southern wall is almost completely covered with white chalk scribbled equations, symbols, phrases, notes, numbers and quotes, and in one corner someone has boldly written the words, "DO NOT ERASE ANY OF THIS!". Hung on the eastern wall are various scrolls, posters, and diagrams of the elements, different magic spells and usages, as well as some star charts and metaphysical maps of magical beings and various constructs. Under the wall hangings is a second long display table stacked with old manuscripts, journals, spell books, spell guides, and the occasional magical prop in the form of totems, wands, and other magical paraphernalia. One could spend hours perusing the different magical materials and mediums kept in this large lecture hall, and still not take it all in. A large brass plaque in the center of one wall reads, "School of Science and Magic." A smaller brass plaque below it reads, "A star is born, another dies. Within the void we see its eyes. A flowing manna from the vein. Through us it bleeds, eternal rain."

The area is illuminated.

[Umbral Rook University - Anatomy and Healing]
Two long metal examining tables center this sterile medical hall. Even the floor in here appears to have been recently mopped, and shines as you walk across it. Lining every wall are large metal cabinets with glass doors and some expose stacks of fresh linen, towels, bandages, tourniquets and all manner of surgical cloth and drapes. Others contain surgical devices both ancient and modern, and all are sterile, clean, and ready for use. One cabinet contains liquid-filled glass jars of organs, brains, eyes, and other body parts which have been preserved for further study. Scattered about the hall are several large wooden tripod-based story boards covered with labeled diagrams, drawings, and layouts of the different anatomically correct bodies of the races and beings of Aedin. Along the western wall is a large steel sink and several wash stations containing water jugs, beakers, towels, and soap. A large brass plaque in the center of one wall reads, "School of Anatomy and Medicine." A smaller brass plaque below it reads, "Our prayers are balanced with skill and knowledge, so that we may save lives."

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: closed cold platinum door, open lecture hall door

[Umbral Rook University - Morgue]
The university morgue and cadaver room is kept at a cool temperature with thick stone walls blocking out all sunlight and heat during the day. Windowless walls are well-lit with steel sconces and five rectangular steel examination tables are draped with white sheets covering what appear to be dead bodies of many different shapes and sizes. As with the medical hall next door, this spacious room is kept immaculately clean at all times. Tall steel cabinets with glass doors line the eastern wall exposing their contents of fresh linen, compresses, bandages, and towels. Along the western wall is a large steel sink and several wash stations complete with water jugs, soap, towels, and several small steel buckets of sterilized surgical knives and tools. A large wooden tripod-based story board holds a labeled and drawn list of surgical saws, knives, scalpels, clamps, and other medical devices used by healers, clerics and doctors throughout the ages of the Isles of Aedin. [Umbral Rook University - Performing Arts]
A small stage on a wooden riser sits along the northern wall in this spacious but cluttered room for the performing arts. At center stage stands a sturdy wood podium for lectures and readings. Several wooden folding chairs are scattered about and face the small stage randomly. To the left of the stage and riser is a small circle of chairs and music stands. A large four string amberwood viol leans precariously against the wall in the corner, and above it is a small chart on the wall depicting musical scales. A jet black grand harpsichord sits to the right of the small stage and it is fully propped open. Posted on every wall are banners of previous shows performed here by the students throughout the years. Against the southern wall is a sink and a make-up station laden with towels, soaps, water jugs, and make-up dishes of various sizes and hues. Next to the sink is a large wheeled costume rack holding dozens of different brightly colored garments, uniforms, costumes and drapes. A large brass plaque in the center of one wall reads, "School of Performance Arts."

[Umbral Rook University - Entrance]
The grand entrance to the sprawling university is mostly made of marble, and elaborately built from top to bottom. Wide stone steps lead up to a white stone double wide walkway, underneath a massive white stone arch. Perched on the very top of the arch is a majestic stone rook spreading his mighty wings and preparing to fly. Where his giant talons still clutch onto the arch, it cracks as if it is breaking, but only aesthetically. The round pillars of the arch are ornately etched with patterns of ivy vines, and a large square brass plaque on each side at the base of the massive arch reads, "The Karishtan Umbral Rook University." The entire entrance is well-lit with tall black cast iron lamp posts which match the decorative black cast iron fence lining each side of the steps, entrance and arch.

The area is illuminated.

[West Academia]
The broad three story mansion to the north is a stately brick building with a wide balcony on each floor. The vaulted entrance is fronted by a set of stone stairs which lead to a split entryway, leading to both the front doors, and a side porch. White pillars support nicely trimmed eaves, awnings, balconies, and window ledges. A small front door on the top floor leads to a small curved outer balcony which sits on the top of the two story, high vaulted entrance. Two large bay windows on the main floor reveal a large parlor within. A Plaque on the arched front door reads, "Master Beregondl, School of Attunement." To the east lies the great library, famous for its collection of books, tomes and other written materials of the ages. The sprawling, open campus of the regal Karishtan Umbral Rook University lies to the west, its enormous grand arch towering above a lush, neatly-manicured front courtyard.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: east, courtyard

e

[Intersect of West Academia and Chassi]
Close to the university to the west, the tall buildings of the sprawling campus begin to come into view. To the south, Chassi dead ends at a small memorial park. A pair of young students from the university pass by while laughing and chatting with one another. Their voices carry in the otherwise stillness of the West Horn, and one could marvel at the peacefulness that permeates this section of Urda.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, east

e

[Intersect of Maelstrom and West Academia]
Maelstrom Main runs north from here and adjacent to the historical library. To the south, a large plot of land has been excavated, and a wide and long stone foundation has been sunk into the ground. Several stone masons toil around the newly laid foundation, chiseling, chipping, and mixing mortar to be used for the brick walls which will sit on top of the foundation. One mason is stacking the newly delivered bricks in small piles at ten foot intervals around the new foundation. A large white sign at the front of the property reads, " Future site of the new Knight's Hall."

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

[Corner of West Academia and Adjudication]
Adjudication dead ends at the south end of the great library. West Academia begins here and runs west to the university. The wide cobblestone streets of the West Horn are clean and orderly. The quiet peacefulness of the West Horn can be attributed to the presence of the guard barracks to the south, or to the jail complex which lies within, and underneath the West Horn. The library and university tend to attract citizens of Urda who are intent on higher learning and enlightenment, instead of mischief and mayhem, thus furthering the tranquility within the West Horn.

The area is illuminated.

[Intersect of Adjudication and East Academia]
East Academia runs eastward towards the main road of the West Horn. To the north, it makes a western turn into West Academia which leads to the university itself. At the intersection, a pair of guards lean against the black cast iron lamp post, idly chatting and eyeballing those who pass by. One spits dark tobacco juice onto the ground, and then chews a mouthful of some dark leaf while looking passersby up and down. Dismissing their presence quickly, he turns back to his friend, chuckles and continues their conversation.

The area is illuminated.

[Adjudication Avenue]
Simple but sturdy black cast iron lamp posts light the cobblestone streets of the West Horn at night. During the day they serve as leaning posts for guards or locals to gather and share news or gossip. To the west lies a lush green garden estate which is centered nicely with a one story white sprawling mansion. White pillars line the front of the entrance and stalwartly support a vaulted entrance made of white stone. The double doors are made of some type of wood, carved intricately into shapes of angels, cherubs, wings, and winged beings, and then painted white to match the mansion. A white picket fence surrounds the estate, and is covered in several places with sweeping green vines of ivy. The white picket gate bears a small wooden plaque which is also painted white, and then painted with small bright flowers and the name, "Mistress Henesay of Karishta".

The area is illuminated.

Through the gate in the distance, the small street begins to make its descent underground, disappearing into a gaping black entrance. The wide cobblestone streets in the West Horn are simple, but clean and well-kept. Scholars, merchants, tradesmen, craftsmen and other local, middle to high-class Urda citizens call the West Horn home. Within its simple square brown structure lies the head guard's office, a small cafeteria, and plenty of barracks to accommodate a small army of guards and sentries. The large wood doors are propped open and guards bustle back and forth from the entrance at all times of the day or night, as their shifts start or end. A small round metal chimney spout on the roof spews out a black trail of smoke which reaches lazily into the sky, and smells of smoking meat. To the south, a paved walkway slopes down into central Urda.

The quiet, peaceful streets of the West Horn are the perfect place for a student from the university to wander in thought, or for a mother to walk with her children without worry. [West Horn Main Gate]
Two square red brick and mortar pillars stand twenty feet high on each side of the well-guarded gate to the West Horn. The elaborate black cast iron double gate is twenty five feet high, with a well designed arch at the top. The elaborate arch is nicely centered with the words, "West Horn of Urda." Winding in and out of the broad black cast iron letters are vines of ivy which make their way down the arch and brick pillars eventually crawling away under the well-trimmed bushes lining the pathway on each side. Patrons of the Karishtan Umbral Rook University and Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library are constantly entering and leaving the West Horn, heads bowed in thought, some with open books, all intent on higher learning. Occasionally a pair of guards pass through the elaborate gate, escorting a prisoner to the city jail, or leaving to find one. A well armed and uniformed guard stands on each side of the gate scrutinizing everyone who passes in or out of the West Horn.

The area is illuminated.

[Corner of Ivy Park]
Ivy Park dead ends at the Skyline Museum of Modern Magic which looms to the north. The great three story hall was rebuilt by the Karishtans with great care and trouble, and the architecture bears the truth of this. New glossy wood beams combine with old stone pillars to complete a majestic structure of square rooms topped with vaulted ceilings, and high arched roof domes far above the streets. Several gold plaques on wooden posts line the front area entrance, and tell of the artifacts, ancient tomes, and other assorted magic antiques, now housed within the mighty building. The doors of the entrance itself are locked, and special permission must be obtained to join regularly scheduled tours of the displays inside. A local guard stands resolutely at the front door and stares off into the distance at nothing in particular. Northern Skyline street runs west from the other side of the museum, blocking passage from Ivy Park.

The area is illuminated.

[Ivy Park - Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library]
Several wide stone steps lead up the the double door entrance of the grand library. Black cast iron hand rails split them in the middle and line them on the outside edges.  On each side of the wide double stairs is a life size bronze statue. The northern one is a human male sitting in a comfortable pose, and reading an open tome. The southern statue is a human female carrying a stack of books in one arm and a torch in the other. The multi-door entrance is well-lit with several black cast iron sconces hung above the doors. The black painted wooden doors swing outward to reveal the myriad of book cases, shelves, tables, chairs, and reading stations housed within.

go door

[Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library - Entrance]
The high vaulted white plaster ceilings are a stark contrast to the deeply stained wood walls. Named after a famous Karishtan Oracle and explorer, Garak Wispelleth, who studied and graduated from the school of history and geography, this massive library and repository is a stunning example of fine Karishtan engineering and building. In the very center of the entrance is the main reception desk. Here a patron can ask for directions, seek a book, check out a book, or purchase something from the small book store. A large glass cabinet next to the reception desk displays journals, ink, quills, book carriers and other assorted small goods as well as a snack or two. The Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library is famous for its collection of reading materials covering most subjects, and from all over the Isles of Aedin. Anyone who has spent time and energy on a higher education has spent many hours studying within the well-restored walls of the great library of knowledge.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: north, open pair of black painted wooden and stained glass doors

go door

[Ivy Park - Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library]
Several wide stone steps lead up the the double door entrance of the grand library. Black cast iron hand rails split them in the middle and line them on the outside edges.  On each side of the wide double stairs is a life size bronze statue. The northern one is a human male sitting in a comfortable pose, and reading an open tome. The southern statue is a human female carrying a stack of books in one arm and a torch in the other. The multi-door entrance is well-lit with several black cast iron sconces hung above the doors. The black painted wooden doors swing outward to reveal the myriad of book cases, shelves, tables, chairs, and reading stations housed within.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north, open pair of black painted wooden and stained glass doors

s

[Ivy Park]
The library itself is a stately three story building made of stone. The architecture of the library is square and vaulted, and wide stone pillars run from the ground to the high roof at intervals of ten feet completely around the square structure. Along the outer eaves of the roof, the crown molding is intricate and carved into the shape of sinuous hydra heads, peaking through carvings of ivy that crawl back and forth along its edge. Tall square windows without curtains look out over Ivy Park, and rows upon rows of shelves filled with books and tomes can be seen within.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

s

[Ivy Park]
No estates line Ivy park as it runs north and south, east of the great library. To the east are several smaller vaulted buildings which serve as warehousing for the library itself, as well as many artisan storefronts. Locked inside are many precious and ancient artifacts, manuscripts, and texts of long lost cultures and peoples, which can be seen through the uncovered, but locked large square windows in each small building. A local sentry is standing at the locked entrance door of each small warehouse, guarding the antiquities within. Patrons are allowed to look through the windows at the many artifacts, but never allowed inside for preservation and security reasons. To the west, the great library of knowledge towers above the streets.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

s

[Intersect of East Academia and Ivy Park]
Ivy Park runs north to the entrance of the library and beyond. On each side of the wide cobblestone street are black cast iron lamp posts topped with four sided glass lanterns.  To the east is the main road of the West Horn, and to the west lies Adjudication Avenue. To the northwest the tall spires of the library stretch skyward with defiance.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

w

[East Academia]
To the north the tall spires of the rebuilt library jut skywards, imposing above a vaulted roof made of black slate shingles. The three level building is made of red brick and stone, and several balconies on each floor overlook the fine grounds of the library estate. Each balcony is supported and encased by white pillars and fronted with black cast iron railings. To the south, the large brown square guard building houses the barracks where the guards live and learn. A guard sentry posted on the roof glares down momentarily at the passing students, and then continues his vigil over the West Horn.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

w

[Intersect of Adjudication and East Academia]
East Academia runs eastward towards the main road of the West Horn. To the north, it makes a western turn into West Academia which leads to the university itself. At the intersection, a pair of guards lean against the black cast iron lamp post, idly chatting and eyeballing those who pass by. One spits dark tobacco juice onto the ground, and then chews a mouthful of some dark leaf while looking passersby up and down. Dismissing their presence quickly, he turns back to his friend, chuckles and continues their conversation.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, east, north

[East Academia]
The northern estate here is a bit more lavish than the other estates in the West Horn. Surrounded by a black cast iron fence which is supported by square brick pillars, it is unwelcoming and foreboding in appearance. Atop the closed and locked black cast iron gate is a plaque which reads, "Master Malvolencia". The two story Tudor-style building is surrounded by a tall hedge of green bushes, making the mansion seem even more impermeable. To the south is the large brown square guard building. To the east is the main road of the West Horn.

[Intersect of West Horn and Academia]
East Academia leads to the Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library or to West Academia, which leads to the Umbral Rook University. The white cobblestone road is always bustling with groups of students conversing in quiet tones as they walk from class to class, or to the library. Some spend their study time on white stone benches lining the street, or sitting on the small square patches of lush green grass in between them, which are very well-tended and trimmed.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, north

n

[West Horn of Urda]
The West horn is patrolled by well-armed guards who march up and down the cobblestone streets. Home to their barracks as well, their presence seems to ward off most crime in the area. Once in a while however, the peace of the West Horn is disturbed by the arrival of a notorious criminal to the jail, or a public execution to be held. Other than the excitement of the jail house, most adventurers would find the West Horn to be a boring place, full of scholars and merchants seeking only a higher education, or a peaceful place to live and raise their children.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[West Horn of Urda]
The well-built but modest homes continue to the east and west along the West Horn. Most of them have chimneys, and a few spout small trails of lazy smoke into the sky. A Silvarin maid opens the front door of a house to the west and stands outside, shaking a small foot rug. She spots the passersby and hurriedly steps back inside, slamming the curved-top wood door behind her. Curiosity overwhelms her then, and she peeks through a small round window in the door, watching them as they pass by.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[West Horn of Urda]
To the west, a one-story rambler made of red brick and mortar, and a tall arching wood roof, centers a square green plot of land. The trim, molding and adornments of the homes in the West Horn are brightly painted with white, light green, mauve, and other pastel colors. To the east a small two-story Tudor is brightly lit inside and all of the doors and windows are open wide. A small sign on the front lawn reads, "Open house", and several possible buyers can be spotted mulling about the property. The land plots in the West Horn are slightly smaller than in the East Horn, and the closeness of the adjacent properties gives the area a nice, snug, peaceful family living environment.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[Intersect of West Horn and Preamble]
The cobblestone streets in the West Horn are well-kept and clean. Simple but orderly, the West Horn is home to many of the city's scholars and local merchants. The modest tudor and rambler-style buildings which line each side of the street are proper, carefully painted and tended to. The yards are large and square, and almost identical in size. The overgrowth, trees, bushes, and flower gardens are trimmed, and constantly cared for by the landowners. The West Horn is well-lit at night by the occasional black cast iron lamp post.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, north

n

[West Horn Main Gate]
Two square red brick and mortar pillars stand twenty feet high on each side of the well-guarded gate to the West Horn. The elaborate black cast iron double gate is twenty five feet high, with a well designed arch at the top. The elaborate arch is nicely centered with the words, "West Horn of Urda." Winding in and out of the broad black cast iron letters are vines of ivy which make their way down the arch and brick pillars eventually crawling away under the well-trimmed bushes lining the pathway on each side. Patrons of the Karishtan Umbral Rook University and Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library are constantly entering and leaving the West Horn, heads bowed in thought, some with open books, all intent on higher learning. Occasionally a pair of guards pass through the elaborate gate, escorting a prisoner to the city jail, or leaving to find one. A well armed and uniformed guard stands on each side of the gate scrutinizing everyone who passes in or out of the West Horn.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, gate

[West Horn of Urda]
A street sweeper toils along one side of the wide cobblestone street, sweeping fallen leaves, twigs and other garbage into a pile beside a garbage can. Several blackbirds make a V-shape in the sky before they swoop in from above and land in the wide branches of a cherry tree. The quiet, peaceful streets of the West Horn are the perfect place for a student from the university to wander in thought, or for a mother to walk with her children without worry. The guard barracks to the west, and the guards themselves provide a strong deterrent to any potential criminal or miscreant behavior. Nestled within and underneath the West Horn, the jail complex also adds to the security and lawfulness within the area.

The area is illuminated.

[West Horn of Urda]
The seven foot red brick and mortar walls which surround the West Horn entirely are occasionally overgrown with vines of ivy, or wild rose bushes which sprawl out in every direction along and down the walls. The small red wild roses belch a yellow fire of interior petals which has given them their name, "Fire Roses." Even the wild overgrowth here appears to have been well-tended, and watered, and it has responded in healthy hues of dark green leaves and perky blossoms, or heavily laden with fruit which thrive unconditionally.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

[Intersect of West Horn and Guardian]
A pair of guards lean on a tall black cast iron lamp post conversing with one another idly. They lazily look over the crowd as it passes by, and then dismissing its presence, return to their unimportant conversation. Guardian Gable is famous for its criminal marches, and public executions. Lining the wide cobblestone street on the south side are several white benches for locals to sit on and watch as the latest chained criminal or miscreant is marched back and forth in public humiliation along the Gable before being led to the jail complex. To the northwest is the large guard barracks, and to the west lies the only entrance to the building itself.

The area is illuminated.

[Guardian Gable]
Several wooden stocks stand imposingly next to the guard house to the north. Used to punish lesser offenses, occasionally a captured thief or bandit has been locked into one for a time, his head and hands protruding out the front through small holes. A bloodstained metal table next to them suggests that repeat offenders may have lost a hand or a limb here, as a handcuff dangles loosely from the edge of the table itself.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

w

[Guardian Gable]
The entrance to the large guard house and barracks is unguarded and open. Only a complete fool would attempt to commit a crime here, as this building is the heart of all guard operations. Within its simple square brown structure lies the head guard's office, a small cafeteria, and plenty of barracks to accommodate a small army of guards and sentries. The large wood doors are propped open and guards bustle back and forth from the entrance at all times of the day or night, as their shifts start or end. A small round metal chimney spout on the roof spews out a black trail of smoke which reaches lazily into the sky, and smells of smoking meat. To the south, a paved walkway slopes down into central Urda.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, east

[West Walkway]
The paved sloping walkway is a model of orderly progression, as beyond being the most popular entryway into the West Horn, it is the path taken by the guards and knights from their bases to the central square. Ornate railings running down the sides and center allow for safe travel as the slope steepens.

The area is illuminated.

[Juniper Grove]

Dainty buttercups sprout up along the trail to the front door of the cottage. He waves to passersby, as well as his neighbors.

[Merchant Square]
Smoke steadily rises from the chimneys of this massive stone building on the north side of the well maintained square. Through the open facade, young Vorngar can be seen pumping the bellows while the master heats a rod of glowing iron within the forge.  The clanking sound of metal on metal can be heard as the gifted apprentices create and sharpen weapons of all types. The hot fires of the forge radiate all around. A large sign mounted above a tall studded iron door clearly reads, "Talafon's Weapons and Armor".  On the south side of the road a small colorful building reflects light cast from two glowing orbs mounted on each side of a glittering portal floating between a silver archway. Above the archway a small, but functional sign reads, "Malinar's Magic and Mayhem".

The area is illuminated.

[Guardian Gable]

Within its simple square brown structure lies the head guard's office, a small cafeteria, and plenty of barracks to accommodate a small army of guards and sentries. The large wood doors are propped open and guards bustle back and forth from the entrance at all times of the day or night, as their shifts start or end. [Vallhaven]

Largely regarded by the guards as the most important post in the West Horn, due to the high ranking fallen who are entombed there, they are always on the alert, even though their vigilance is seldom tested. A large brass plaque atop a wood stand is inscribed with the history of the West Horn and the cemetery itself.

[Chassi Grove]

A small ivory water fountain sits in the center of the cobblestone lane, cheerfully bubbling with clear clean water.

[Chassi Amphitheater]
Here at the end of Chassi Grove, a large outdoor amphitheater has been built for lectures, gatherings, and other shows of interest. Named after a famous Karishtan actress who once attended the school of performing arts at the university, it is a wide stone testament to Karishtan engineering. Occasionally a musician will perform here, or a traveling side show will occupy the outdoor venue. The long benches in the seating area look as if they could accommodate about two hundred patrons at full capacity. The rear benches have been placed on wooden risers for better viewing of the main staging area. Standing twenty five feet tall in the very center of the staging area is a bronze statue of Karishta, Goddess of knowledge. She wields a divine lantern in one hand, and a thick tome in the other. Her brow is stern, and her eyes are keenly squinted into the sky, as if she still searches for answers. The amphitheater and secluded grove are completely silent, and even the local birds seem to quiet themselves within this sacred hollow.

The area is illuminated.

[Intersect of Maelstrom and West Academia]
Maelstrom Main runs north from here and adjacent to the historical library. To the south, a large plot of land has been excavated, and a wide and long stone foundation has been sunk into the ground. Several stone masons toil around the newly laid foundation, chiseling, chipping, and mixing mortar to be used for the brick walls which will sit on top of the foundation. One mason is stacking the newly delivered bricks in small piles at ten foot intervals around the new foundation. A large white sign at the front of the property reads, " Future site of the new Knight's Hall."

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

n

[Maelstrom Main]
A sprawling green estate and two-story wood rambler to the west is completely surrounded with a decorative white cast iron patterned fence. The slim rails are finely twisted, and a large section of them disappears under heavily laden wild grape vines. The fat purple berries hang low in clumps, testing the strength of the lifeline of their vines. Broad green leaves adorn each clump, partially sheltering it from too much sun, or too much weather. The tall arched white cast iron entrance gate of the property leads to a nicely patterned cobblestone walkway which leads to the domed entryway itself. A plaque on the front gate reads,  "Matriarch Bengalia, School of Theology." To the east, the great library and surrounding grounds quietly beckon to the purposeful student, as a place of undisturbed study and learning.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[Maelstrom Main]
A single story white brick house sits on a green estate to the west. A large outdoor porch starts at the front door and juts around the north side of the building, eventually coming to an end at the side door and entrance. The grand porch is covered with a wooden slate roof and awning, and nicely decorated with lattice half walls extending down to a grand white railing which completes the outdoor seating area. Several outdoor wooden chairs sit in random places on the porch, and a double wide chair swing hangs from the entrance beam next to the white painted front door itself. The plaque attached to the front gate reads, "Honorable Judge Satvias, School of Law." To the east towers the great library, where some of the brightest minds of the Isles of Aedin have spent endless hours in deep study and research.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[Maelstrom Main]
Maelstrom Main is home to many of the professors who teach at the university as shown by their names on each entrance gate. The two story Villa to the west is stained in deep mahogany, and tall arched roof eaves cover a stately round entrance which leads to a tall arched double door. The plaque on the front gate reads, "Professor Schenker, School of Anatomy and Healing." A bronze statue of a robed cleric holding a divining bowl in one hand and a scalpel in the other, stands soberly on the front lawn. The great library to the east of the building is surrounded by green lawns and well-trimmed bushes. A pair of students from the university are sitting under one of the elm trees which have been planted carefully at intervals of every ten feet around the repository. They are both engrossed in books and papers and oblivious to the presence of others as they pass them by.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[Corner of Maelstrom and Northern Skyline]
Maelstrom Main runs south from here towards West Academia. Northern Skyline is a cross section of old restored structures, as well as brand new ones. Some are still being built, and construction dust and debris covers much of the newly cut cobblestone street. A large bronze statue of Karishta is slowly being delivered to the northern estate, and erected by a team of burly workers. Using various thick ropes and pulleys, they pull it into place and begin to bring it vertical as others quickly pass by and out of their way. The estate itself appears to be a one square sprawling brick and wood rambler on top of a newly planted, fresh green garden estate. The large curtain-less bay windows reveal the emptiness of the brand new interior, still waiting for its owner to move in.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, east

e

[Northern Skyline]
To the north is a large white and lavender manor sitting on a wide green estate. It appears to have been converted to a student dormitory, as evidenced by the young students milling in and out of the property. A bulky female Fae stands inside a circle of seated female students, and they stare at her in silence as she lectures them on an unknown subject. A pair of male human students casually lean on the fence surrounding the dorms, watching the female group with interest and chuckling quietly to each other. A local guard sits on a chair by the front gate, fast asleep. To the south the great library is lined with well manicured bushes and lush green trees. The large square windows of the library are uncovered, and several patrons can be seen inside reading, resting, studying, or staring outside dreamily.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

e

[Northern Skyline]
A new building is being erected to the north, and several builders are toiling around it. A stone mason sweats over a red porridge of mortar, while a carpenter saws on a long beam propped on a wooden sawhorse. A large sweaty shirtless human barks orders at someone inside the gaping unfinished front entrance, and someone curses from inside. The job foreman then stomps inside and disappears from view. A large white sign placed at the front of the property reads, "The future site of the Windgard Planetarium Plaza." To the south, the great library that houses books and tomes and other written material from all over the Isles of Aedin can be seen. Scholars from Windgard and other isles come here to study, read and elevate their education and knowledge in conjunction with classes from the university.

The area is illuminated.

[West Academia]
The broad three story mansion to the north is a stately brick building with a wide balcony on each floor. The vaulted entrance is fronted by a set of stone stairs which lead to a split entryway, leading to both the front doors, and a side porch. White pillars support nicely trimmed eaves, awnings, balconies, and window ledges. A small front door on the top floor leads to a small curved outer balcony which sits on the top of the two story, high vaulted entrance. Two large bay windows on the main floor reveal a large parlor within. A Plaque on the arched front door reads, "Master Beregondl, School of Attunement." To the east lies the great library, famous for its collection of books, tomes and other written materials of the ages. The sprawling, open campus of the regal Karishtan Umbral Rook University lies to the west, its enormous grand arch towering above a lush, neatly-manicured front courtyard.

One spits dark tobacco juice onto the ground, and then chews a mouthful of some dark leaf while looking passersby up and down. Dismissing their presence quickly, he turns back to his friend, chuckles and continues their conversation.

The three level building is made of red brick and stone, and several balconies on each floor overlook the fine grounds of the library estate. Each balcony is supported and encased by white pillars and fronted with black cast iron railings. To the south, the large brown square guard building houses the barracks where the guards live and learn. A guard sentry posted on the roof glares down momentarily at the passing students, and then continues his vigil over the West Horn.

Surrounded by a black cast iron fence which is supported by square brick pillars, it is unwelcoming and foreboding in appearance. Atop the closed and locked black cast iron gate is a plaque which reads, "Master Malvolencia". The two story Tudor-style building is surrounded by a tall hedge of green bushes, making the mansion seem even more impermeable. To the south is the large brown square guard building. To the east is the main road of the West Horn.

[Intersect of West Horn and Academia]
East Academia leads to the Wispelleth-Karisian Historical Library or to West Academia, which leads to the Umbral Rook University. The white cobblestone road is always bustling with groups of students conversing in quiet tones as they walk from class to class, or to the library. Some spend their study time on white stone benches lining the street, or sitting on the small square patches of lush green grass in between them, which are very well-tended and trimmed.

The area is illuminated.

[West Horn of Urda]
To the southwest looms a large square building with square, uncovered windows, bare of any curtains or shutters. The building is well-lit inside and out, and several burly guards are seen milling about the area, or entering the building itself. To the east and south, the modest well-kept houses continue to line the wide cobblestone street.

The area is illuminated.

s

[West Horn of Urda]
A street sweeper toils along one side of the wide cobblestone street, sweeping fallen leaves, twigs and other garbage into a pile beside a garbage can. Several blackbirds make a V-shape in the sky before they swoop in from above and land in the wide branches of a cherry tree. The quiet, peaceful streets of the West Horn are the perfect place for a student from the university to wander in thought, or for a mother to walk with her children without worry. The guard barracks to the west, and the guards themselves provide a strong deterrent to any potential criminal or miscreant behavior. Nestled within and underneath the West Horn, the jail complex also adds to the security and lawfulness within the area.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

s

[West Horn of Urda]
The seven foot red brick and mortar walls which surround the West Horn entirely are occasionally overgrown with vines of ivy, or wild rose bushes which sprawl out in every direction along and down the walls. The small red wild roses belch a yellow fire of interior petals which has given them their name, "Fire Roses." Even the wild overgrowth here appears to have been well-tended, and watered, and it has responded in healthy hues of dark green leaves and perky blossoms, or heavily laden with fruit which thrive unconditionally.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

s

[Intersect of West Horn and Guardian]
A pair of guards lean on a tall black cast iron lamp post conversing with one another idly. They lazily look over the crowd as it passes by, and then dismissing its presence, return to their unimportant conversation. Guardian Gable is famous for its criminal marches, and public executions. Lining the wide cobblestone street on the south side are several white benches for locals to sit on and watch as the latest chained criminal or miscreant is marched back and forth in public humiliation along the Gable before being led to the jail complex. To the northwest is the large guard barracks, and to the west lies the only entrance to the building itself.

The area is illuminated.

[Guardian Gable]
Several wooden stocks stand imposingly next to the guard house to the north. Used to punish lesser offenses, occasionally a captured thief or bandit has been locked into one for a time, his head and hands protruding out the front through small holes. A bloodstained metal table next to them suggests that repeat offenders may have lost a hand or a limb here, as a handcuff dangles loosely from the edge of the table itself.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

w

[Guardian Gable]
The entrance to the large guard house and barracks is unguarded and open. Only a complete fool would attempt to commit a crime here, as this building is the heart of all guard operations. Within its simple square brown structure lies the head guard's office, a small cafeteria, and plenty of barracks to accommodate a small army of guards and sentries. The large wood doors are propped open and guards bustle back and forth from the entrance at all times of the day or night, as their shifts start or end. A small round metal chimney spout on the roof spews out a black trail of smoke which reaches lazily into the sky, and smells of smoking meat. To the south, a paved walkway slopes down into central Urda.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, east

[Intersect of Guardian and Adjudication]
Guardian Gable meets Adjudication Avenue which runs north to the Library or the University. To the west lies a small decorative fenced graveyard. To the northwest is a small fence-lined side street leading to the underground entrance of the jail complex. The high barbed front gate of the side street is closed and firmly locked. Through the gate in the distance, the small street begins to make its descent underground, disappearing into a gaping black entrance. The wide cobblestone streets in the West Horn are simple, but clean and well-kept. Scholars, merchants, tradesmen, craftsmen and other local, middle to high-class Urda citizens call the West Horn home. Quiet and peaceful, only the local birds and other wildlife make any noise throughout the suburban area. Except on public execution day...

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

[Guardian Gable]
At the end of Guardian Gable is the small cemetery where many locals have been buried or entombed. A pleasant little meadow surrounded by birch trees in a square plot of land is dotted with upright tombstones and small assorted, well-trimmed, green leaf bushes. A local human gardener can be seen trimming around a headstone inside the quiet peaceful park of the dead.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: east, white stone arch

A freshly dug grave bears no coffin, and still smells of fresh, moist dirt. Sitting on the fresh pile of dirt next to the grave is a shovel and a pickaxe.

To the west is a stately two story manor with tall arched bay windows and eaves. Two square brick chimneys jut up from the roof on each side, but no smoke comes from within. [Adjudication Avenue]

A uniformed bow wielding sentry can be seen marching back and forth along the edge of the barracks roof above you. He quickly makes eye contact with you before continuing to march and survey the area.

The area is illuminated.

n

[Adjudication Avenue]
Simple but sturdy black cast iron lamp posts light the cobblestone streets of the West Horn at night. During the day they serve as leaning posts for guards or locals to gather and share news or gossip. To the west lies a lush green garden estate which is centered nicely with a one story white sprawling mansion. White pillars line the front of the entrance and stalwartly support a vaulted entrance made of white stone. The double doors are made of some type of wood, carved intricately into shapes of angels, cherubs, wings, and winged beings, and then painted white to match the mansion. A white picket fence surrounds the estate, and is covered in several places with sweeping green vines of ivy. The white picket gate bears a small wooden plaque which is also painted white, and then painted with small bright flowers and the name, "Mistress Henesay of Karishta".

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

n

[Intersect of Adjudication and East Academia]
East Academia runs eastward towards the main road of the West Horn. To the north, it makes a western turn into West Academia which leads to the university itself. At the intersection, a pair of guards lean against the black cast iron lamp post, idly chatting and eyeballing those who pass by. One spits dark tobacco juice onto the ground, and then chews a mouthful of some dark leaf while looking passersby up and down. Dismissing their presence quickly, he turns back to his friend, chuckles and continues their conversation.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, east, north

n

[Corner of West Academia and Adjudication]
Adjudication dead ends at the south end of the great library. West Academia begins here and runs west to the university. The wide cobblestone streets of the West Horn are clean and orderly. The quiet peacefulness of the West Horn can be attributed to the presence of the guard barracks to the south, or to the jail complex which lies within, and underneath the West Horn. The library and university tend to attract citizens of Urda who are intent on higher learning and enlightenment, instead of mischief and mayhem, thus furthering the tranquility within the West Horn.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south

w

[Intersect of Maelstrom and West Academia]
Maelstrom Main runs north from here and adjacent to the historical library. To the south, a large plot of land has been excavated, and a wide and long stone foundation has been sunk into the ground. Several stone masons toil around the newly laid foundation, chiseling, chipping, and mixing mortar to be used for the brick walls which will sit on top of the foundation. One mason is stacking the newly delivered bricks in small piles at ten foot intervals around the new foundation. A large white sign at the front of the property reads, " Future site of the new Knight's Hall."

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

w

[Intersect of West Academia and Chassi]
Close to the university to the west, the tall buildings of the sprawling campus begin to come into view. To the south, Chassi dead ends at a small memorial park. A pair of young students from the university pass by while laughing and chatting with one another. Their voices carry in the otherwise stillness of the West Horn, and one could marvel at the peacefulness that permeates this section of Urda.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, east

w

[West Academia]
The broad three story mansion to the north is a stately brick building with a wide balcony on each floor. The vaulted entrance is fronted by a set of stone stairs which lead to a split entryway, leading to both the front doors, and a side porch. White pillars support nicely trimmed eaves, awnings, balconies, and window ledges. A small front door on the top floor leads to a small curved outer balcony which sits on the top of the two story, high vaulted entrance. Two large bay windows on the main floor reveal a large parlor within. A Plaque on the arched front door reads, "Master Beregondl, School of Attunement." To the east lies the great library, famous for its collection of books, tomes and other written materials of the ages. The sprawling, open campus of the regal Karishtan Umbral Rook University lies to the west, its enormous grand arch towering above a lush, neatly-manicured front courtyard.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: east, courtyard

e

s

[Adjudication Avenue]
Simple but sturdy black cast iron lamp posts light the cobblestone streets of the West Horn at night. During the day they serve as leaning posts for guards or locals to gather and share news or gossip. To the west lies a lush green garden estate which is centered nicely with a one story white sprawling mansion. White pillars line the front of the entrance and stalwartly support a vaulted entrance made of white stone. The double doors are made of some type of wood, carved intricately into shapes of angels, cherubs, wings, and winged beings, and then painted white to match the mansion. A white picket fence surrounds the estate, and is covered in several places with sweeping green vines of ivy. The white picket gate bears a small wooden plaque which is also painted white, and then painted with small bright flowers and the name, "Mistress Henesay of Karishta".

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north


[Adjudication Avenue]
Adjudication continues north and south while estates and homes line the western side of the cobblestone road. To the east looms the large guard barracks. A uniformed bow wielding sentry can be seen marching back and forth along the edge of the barracks roof above you. He quickly makes eye contact with you before continuing to march and survey the area.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north


[Adjudication Avenue]
The large guard barracks to the east is well-lit, and several of the large square uncovered windows are open wide. A local guard glares down from a second story window while enjoying a cigar, and leaning out the window sill further, he peers at travelers for a moment. To the west is a stately two story manor with tall arched bay windows and eaves. Two square brick chimneys jut up from the roof on each side, but no smoke comes from within. The ornate silver front gate is open wide, and on a steel plaque above it reads the name, "General Starbourne". A tall silver flagpole in the ground in front of the estate waves a bright flag bearing the guard arms, and the arms of West Urda.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

s

[Intersect of Guardian and Adjudication]
Guardian Gable meets Adjudication Avenue which runs north to the Library or the University. To the west lies a small decorative fenced graveyard. To the northwest is a small fence-lined side street leading to the underground entrance of the jail complex. The high barbed front gate of the side street is closed and firmly locked. Through the gate in the distance, the small street begins to make its descent underground, disappearing into a gaping black entrance. The wide cobblestone streets in the West Horn are simple, but clean and well-kept. Scholars, merchants, tradesmen, craftsmen and other local, middle to high-class Urda citizens call the West Horn home. Quiet and peaceful, only the local birds and other wildlife make any noise throughout the suburban area. Except on public execution day...

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

e

[Guardian Gable]
Several wooden stocks stand imposingly next to the guard house to the north. Used to punish lesser offenses, occasionally a captured thief, or bandit has been locked into one for a time, his head and hands protruding out the front through small holes. A bloodstained metal table next to them suggests that repeat offenders may have lost a hand or a limb here, as a handcuff dangles loosely from the edge of the table itself. To the south is a one story rambler made of gray brick and stone. A small plaque on the decorative gate in front of the home reads, "House of Hurin'gar."

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

e

[Guardian Gable]
The entrance to the large guard house and barracks is unguarded and open. Only a complete fool would attempt to commit a crime here, as this building is the heart of all guard operations. Within its simple square brown structure lies the head guard's office, a small cafeteria, and plenty of barracks to accommodate a small army of guards and sentries. The large wood doors are propped open and guards bustle back and forth from the entrance at all times of the day or night, as their shifts start or end. A small round metal chimney spout on the roof spews out a black trail of smoke which reaches lazily into the sky, and smells of smoking meat. To the south, a paved walkway slopes down into central Urda.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, east

s

[West Walkway]
The paved sloping walkway is a model of orderly progression, as beyond being the most popular entryway into the West Horn, it is the path taken by the guards and knights from their bases to the central square. Ornate railings running down the sides and center allow for safe travel as the slope steepens.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: south, north

s

[Juniper Grove]
The pleasant, stringent aroma of crushed juniper berries lingers in the slight breeze. While some homes may appear finished within their plots in the tree grove, others seem to be under construction still, either without roofs, or unpainted with untended lawns.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, north

[Juniper Grove]
Chimney smoke billows out of a tiny stone chimney atop a grass-thatched cottage. The homeowner appears to be repairing the roof, as he arduously ascends a ladder with sheaves of dried long grass in a wicker pack on his back. Dainty buttercups sprout up along the trail to the front door of the cottage. He waves to passersby, as well as his neighbors.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

e

[Juniper Grove]
A white-washed iron bench has been placed in a corner of the grove, carefully sat atop a brick landing so as not to sink into the ground. The soft trill of the birds and the rustle of the trees has been enough for one pedestrian who sits, dozing away in the bench seat.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

e

[Juniper Grove]
Homes and cottages are more difficult to see as the growth of trees and junipers is more dense here. The gravel pathway is so hard-packed that fresh rocks have been lain down. The new gravel occasionally causes a misstep in travelers, but the grassy road sides would be enough for anyone to rest, or fall into.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east

e

[Juniper Grove]
The majestic height of bushy juniper trees aptly marks the grove for which it was named. Small cottages, some complete, and some still being built, are apparent through the shade and foliage of the many trees. Birds dart from branch to branch, trilling at one another in a sing-song manner. A large orange tent is here.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, east, tent

e

[Northern Cross Road]
The smooth stone walkway of the main road continues to the north, leading outside of the city of Urda. Pedestrians and wagons bustle by towards the south, venturing to the heart of the city and the thriving trading central of the Merchant Run.

The area is illuminated.

Obvious exits are: west, south, east, north
